Failed first-fix focused/checkpoint/verify logs are retained; final successful regressions and completed review are identified explicitly in the checkpoint.

### c4-approved-appliance-child-penetration - body-only fixing approval hides the lowest part
- Observed during approved-root follow-through: both coffee bodies move -10 mm in model z; their rigid drip trays move with them to -2.110 m against modeled worktop -2.098 m, 12 mm penetration. Real before/after child meshes are frozen by value in `out/c4-phase2f/mounting-report.json` and the retained package-e scene. Should have been caught at assembly support selection.
- Cause/escape/class: support datum was chosen from the inventoried body, omitting its lower tray. The child was not a mounting inventory site, so body-only contact passed. Siblings: both coffee machines, attached tanks/spouts, hood chimney, future attached parts.
- Tier 2: `scene_findings` checks every declared associated child against its root?s actual finished support half-space, without identifier exemptions. A tier-1 change to the whole assembly?s lowest fixing part would alter the frozen approved body pose and requires new lead approval; it is not applied.
- Proof: `test_joinery_recess_lights_and_support_siblings_fail_closed` retains both real 12 mm findings; a renamed child lifted to exact support stays quiet, and a new 20 mm penetration fires. Rigid child and emitter displacements are independently checked.
- OPEN: both approved positions and rigid geometry remain as authorized; installation pose needs correction after lead review. Registry: `knowledge/mounting-guards.json`; workflow requires complete assembly review before approving its fixing plane.
- Final lead authority supersedes the historical pose: both complete coffee assemblies lift 12 mm and retain all part relationships. Linux final mounting findings are zero; the frozen former penetrations still fire in focused tests. See `docs/c4-final-linux-checkpoint.md`; installed/native/render proof remains pending.

### c4-geometry-container-comparison - identical support coordinates counted as changed
- Observed: first delivery comparison counted three unchanged stair-floor diagnostic faces because in-memory points were tuples while the retained JSON used lists. Coordinates, polygon order and bounds were identical. Class: representation equality substituted for geometric equality; related to package-e view-ordinal comparison.
- Tier 1: checkpoint `geometry_diff` normalizes point containers before comparing coordinate values. `test_geometry_diff_compares_coordinates_not_container_types` stays quiet on tuple/list siblings and fires on an unrelated 5 mm floor-coordinate mutation. The regenerated comparison records 52 changed prior mesh records, zero changed family meshes and unchanged cameras. Registry: `knowledge/mounting-guards.json`.

### c4-final-rigid-assembly-and-panel-span ? final lead decisions
- Capture: retained package-f scene has two 12 mm coffee-tray penetrations and a 47 mm headboard overrun. Frozen by value in `tests/fixtures/c4-final-before.json`; final authority in `knowledge/c4-final-approvals.json`. Found at lead candidate review; assembly construction should have prevented the split/support error.
- Cause/escape/class: support selection used an appliance body rather than the complete attached assembly; ownership was inferred during one approval path rather than declared by the builder. The headboard used the structural room span before finite finished-wall coverage. Siblings: both coffee trays, spouts and tanks, hood chimney, wall panels on finite returns.
- Diagnosis checkpoint: bind attached parts at construction, translate through one assembly function, seat the lowest retained part on the measured worktop, and trim the panel about the unchanged bed centreline against a full-height source patch. Lead authorized all 14 revised exterior movements and these final corrections.
- Tier 1: `attached_assembly.bind/translate` owns rigid movement, replacing approval-time identifier inference. `final_mounting` seats the complete assembly and bounds panel width by its finite wall span. Automatic small corrections in `fitting_mounting.package` also carry attached parts, with a real coffee/hood regression; supplied package children are deferred to their own package iteration and never moved twice; the mixed-package regression proves this. Tier 2: unconditional split-movement detection joins `scene_findings`. No tolerance, wall, tray shape, camera or bed change.
- Proofs: `tests/test_final_mounting.py` freezes actual collisions/overrun, mutates parent-only translations of both coffee bodies and the hood, keeps rigid siblings quiet, and translates/renames assemblies and panel supports. Final candidate must have no mounting findings and retain exactly three family-bath clearances. Focused process evidence and preview review recorded in the final checkpoint.
- First-fix API rejection: positive generated child projections cannot use `surface-mounted` (it requires zero offset). The construction now uses the existing projected-part contract, as `fitting_mounting.package` does; zero-projection tray retains surface mounting. The failed first focused run is preserved separately. The automatic-move follow-through also exposed the standalone `package` API: its threshold regression passes members separately with no scene mesh inventory. Attached-child lookup now treats that absent inventory as empty; `test_bounded_movement_threshold_generalises` retains both sides of the 5 mm limit.
- Installation/native/photoreal/lighting acceptance stays pending; this closes candidate geometry only after final verification.

### c4-plant-support-legacy-datum — stale check after measured support migration
- Observed in final C4 focused `test_render_standard`: `test_indoor_plants_have_integrated_pots_floor_support_and_clear_tv` reports support failures for lounge-plant, living-plant-table, living-plant, bedroom-plant and study-plant. Reproduced unchanged in retained package-f and final scenes: `out/c4-final-indoor-plant-baseline.json`.
- Direct cause/escape/class: `indoor_plant_violations` compares positions after measured support migration with authored `LZ` room levels and pre-migration furniture heights. All five support comparisons differ by +2 mm; floor plants have measured finished support at -2.998 or +0.002 m, and the table plant is at -2.598 m. The legacy check still expects -3.000, 0.000 and -2.600 m. This is a stale datum check, not evidence to lower the plants. Siblings: post-migration props on floor or furniture; related `c4-support-chain-contracts`.
- OPEN: no plant geometry or tolerance changed. A future fix must compare against actual scene support geometry and prove both real clean cases and sunk/moved support mutations, retaining the existing placement/route checks. Captured for the lead rather than silently exempted.
- Reproduced again on Linux, unchanged in reconstructed package-f and final scenes: `out/c4-final-linux-indoor-plant-baseline.json`. Focused render/lighting modules pass 72 of 73 tests, exit 1; the sole failing test is this known stale-datum check.
- Final C4 test expectations also needed updating: whole-scene mounting findings are now empty, and exterior grilles fix with their minimum model-y face at the facade, projecting 12 mm outdoors. Frozen bad mounting cases and exterior-side mutations remain active.

- Lead diagnosis (Linux, 2026-10-05): commit `4245c7b` added package-e `support_mounting.migrate`. The existing `villa_render` floor constructor already emitted faces at room level plus 0.002 m; migration seated floor plants there and raised the coffee table (and its plant) by 0.002 m. No new floor thickness is inferred. The table plant also copied the table child's floor-bearing mounting contract instead of recording its actual tabletop contact. The original guard ran inside `props` before migration and was never rerun after it; its final-scene test exposed the stale authored levels.
- Found by / stage: focused render regression, then lead review; should have been caught at post-migration scene construction. Class: dependent placement and validation keep authored or parent-root datums after physical supports move. Siblings audited: all five indoor plants, four floor supports and the coffee-table top; imported models/cloth and other props retain their existing carried-movement guards. Related: `c4-support-chain-contracts`, `indoor-pot-support-tv`.
- Frozen reproduction: `tests/fixtures/c4-plant-support-before.json` holds all five actual plants and six physical source meshes by value. `test_c4_frozen_plant_support_datums` first reproduced all five failures (exit 1; `out/c4-plant-support-reproduction.log`), before the source change.
- Control tiers 1 and 2: `plant_support_face` selects the finite upward face of the explicitly named floor/furniture support; plant migration seats and binds directly to that face, rather than inferring an enclosing assembly or copying its floor contract. `plant_support_findings` compares base and recorded datum with live physical geometry. The final builder reruns the pot/support/TV guard with the scene; `mounting.scene_findings` invokes the support check unconditionally, including from `verify.py`. Migrated props without scene geometry fail closed. The 1 mm tolerance and all visible geometry are unchanged.
- Proofs: the frozen real table-root contract fires; clean rebindings stay quiet with unchanged positions. Every plant fires on a 2 mm sunk base, moved/removed physical support and stale-record mutation. Translated/renamed room, furniture and floor siblings pass, then an injected 2 mm base defect fires. Pot and television-corridor failures remain guarded. First-fix focused modules: 67 tests, exit 0 (`out/c4-plant-support-first-fix.log`). `out/c4-plant-support-source-proof.json` confirms identical visible mesh geometry and all five plant positions. No visible change requires a new presentation preview; existing render acceptance remains pending.
- Registry: `knowledge/mounting-guards.json`, lesson `c4-plant-support-legacy-datum`. Workflow and canonical villa-render skill updated together; synchronized adapter regenerated. Candidate source defect fixed; full execution results follow in the lead checkpoint, without closing native/render gates.

### c4-linux-library-paths — transferred index paths prevent Linux verification
- Observed: Linux `verify.py` and focused scene setup fail before review because the transferred index stores `iguzzini\\LSEVO-AAK3EW\\LSEVO-AAK3EW.ldt`. Evidence: initial `out/c4-final-linux-verify.log` and focused logs; frozen selected-product path fields in `tests/fixtures/c4-linux-library-paths.json`. Found during workstation continuation; should be prevented at index serialization and reading.
- Cause/escape/class: `str(relative_path)` serialized the importing operating system's separators; read APIs joined those strings directly to a native path. Earlier tests imported and consumed their own temporary index on the same operating system. Class: persisted relative paths acquire platform-specific filesystem meaning. Siblings: all indexed photometric files and Revit family folders consumed through `get` and `search`, including `install.resolve`.
- Diagnosis checkpoint: write index paths with forward slashes and normalize legacy separators at the common row-read boundary. Do not rebuild or modify the shared library, product files or selected photometry.
- Control tier 1: `rebuild_index` serializes relative paths with `as_posix`; `get` and `search` decode old separators through `_portable_row`, so photometric export and family resolution share the same portable boundary. The index remains unchanged during reading.
- Proofs: `LibraryTests.test_transferred_windows_index_paths_export_and_install_without_reindexing` fails before the fix on the actual frozen selected-product paths, exports all three siblings from temporary files after the fix, resolves their family folders, preserves database bytes and checks unrelated mixed-separator paths. Existing same-platform import/install tests remain quiet. Focused library module: 11 tests, exit 0. Registry: `knowledge/mounting-guards.json`; Linux delivery runs recorded in the checkpoint.

### 2026-10-05 — c4-finished-layer-bookkeeping-only (C4i diagnosis)

- Lead measured 51 unsupported islands on 9069c24. Bathroom fixing planes were 23 mm ahead of visible marble; every nonzero plaster/marble host is a sibling. Must be caught in scene construction.
- Cause: render bookkeeping rejection removed diagnostic fixing planes without constructing the finish assembly. Escape: finite mounting coverage accepted diagnostic geometry; it did not check the render building surface. Class: a correct finished datum without physical finish geometry.
- Control/proofs: C4i adds closed, opening-clipped solids from measured source polygons and a render-only finished-face distance guard; suppression of real layers reproduces the failure. Evidence and final results: `docs/c4i-report.md`.
- Three regression siblings: diagnostic source lookup used the render channel; frozen missing-label reproduction stopped replacing its target after channel migration; final authorized headboard/coffee changes lacked movement records and were compared with earlier approval poses. Preserve channel contracts and immutable movement history.
- C4i sibling probe found a partial-window clip: intersecting a keyhole at a window edge left a repeated slit and nonmanifold extrusion. The original real wall zones passed, but this class needed a polygon-topology repair before extrusion; add an opening-edge clip proof and reject disconnected clipped contours rather than silently exporting a partial solid.
- Final construction: 18 distinct solids; shared guest-WC/dirty-kitchen shell polygons are partitioned by finish zone, supplemental coplanar polygons are room-bounded, and already-finished stair faces are not offset twice. Exact source coverage replaces source faces without coincident interfaces. Tier 2 render-only guard rejects missing oriented finished surfaces within 2 mm; topology guard checks closed edges and outward winding.
- Proofs: real construction suppression yields 51 unsupported islands and 52 missing render-surface mesh IDs; clean scene yields zero of either. Keyhole windows, opening-edge notches, reversed normals, translated geometry, removed faces and reversed winding are tested; disconnected clips refuse before export. Rail/bracket contact uses actual rail-side triangles against the solid brackets: sampling only the rail's end-ring vertices falsely measured 22 mm separation despite the bracket crossing the rail side.
- Independent render-critic review accepts the neutral marble/bracket preview, with faint contact shadows and unchanged procedural faceting disclosed. The preview geometry matches the final candidate exactly. Results and retained logs: `docs/c4i-report.md`, `out/c4i/`. Native/presentation/lighting acceptance remains separate.

### 2026-10-06 — c4-sanitary-wall-relocation

- Capture: client chose east-wall family WC after the west-wall basin access strip intersected its pan. Frozen actual west assembly and host: `tests/fixtures/c4-family-west-before.json`; original three failed rows retained in `out/c4-family-east/before-scene.json` review.
- Cause/escape/class: historical sanitary layout put neighbouring bodies on one wall without constructing full access zones; body/front-only review missed the side and basin-strip requirements. A wall change also risks stale rotation, flush plate, host and native spec. Siblings: all sanitary assemblies and opposing-wall layouts; related `c4-full-bath-access-zones`.
- Tier 1: `sanitary_relocation.relocate` rigidly carries every generated member and rebinds via C4; current layout and native parts share one pose, with explicit historical input scope for frozen mounting approvals. Measured east host must match current pose. Tier 2: full review retains enclosed-shower obstacles regardless of their low height and checks wet-zone non-intersection. No soil-stack or concealed cistern shape invented; services coordination pending.
- Proofs, inspected diagnostic preview, focused exits and remaining native/render evidence are recorded in `docs/c4-family-east-report.md`.

- First proofs caught two assumptions: native pan/seat are separate boxes while the render body combines them; open level-access wet floor cannot be treated as an enclosure under the existing overlapping-zone guidance. Native assembly-union comparison and the open/enclosed distinction preserve real geometry and source applicability. The shower entry card is tested on its 1630 mm width and 780 mm clear-floor depth, not misapplied to interior depth. First focused exit 1 retained; final 57-test process, 44-test furnishing/evidence process (one unavailable-original skip), four-test final relocation rerun, default support/openings two-test process, portable verify and checkpoint all exit 0.
### c4-lens-basis-missing-vertical — a low subject's lens constraint was reported as horizontal
- Observed: full-suite lead found `test_camera_24mm_level_at_eye_height` failing on v35: recorded horizontal need 17.2 degrees, below the 24 mm horizontal half-frame 36.87 degrees. Old camera: position [9.727, -24.971, 1.35], target [12.025, -26.899, 1.35], lens 16 mm. Evidence: `out/c4-family-wc-camera-before.json`, `out/c4-family-wc-lens-before.log`.
- Found by / stage: full regression after east-wall WC relocation; should have been caught at view selection and its focused camera-standard test.
- Reproduction: `tests/fixtures/c4-family-wc-lens-before.json` freezes the actual layout, furniture, walls, columns and specification by value, before the lens-report fix.
- Diagnosis: every searched 24 mm standing point misses the low WC top vertically; best point [9.627, -24.871] needs 28.14 degrees below level eye, beyond the 26.57 degree vertical half-frame. Zero fully framed 24 mm candidate evaluations; minimum vertical excess 1.57 degrees.
- Direct cause: chooser includes low subject height in its miss score, but the exporter reports only horizontal corner angle except for a guest-rain-head identifier special case.
- Escape: the lens-standard regression parses prose and assumes every other wider view failed horizontally. The explanation and assertion do not share the chooser's actual framing criteria.
- Class and siblings: lens escalation diagnostics omit a framing constraint. Every room-selected view can fail horizontally or vertically; the guest rain head is the existing upper-frame sibling, and ordinary low furniture is the lower-frame sibling. Related: `c3-part-boundary` and the historical v29 lens override record.
- A second real defect found during diagnosis: projecting the unchanged built pan and flush plate showed a bottom vertex 40.05 degrees below the old 16 mm camera's axis, beyond its 36.87 degree vertical half-frame. The former height test checked only the top, using radial distance rather than forward camera depth, and the view-plan script checked only horizontal footprints. Class: a subject's visible top is mistaken for whole-object framing. The WC kind, including renamed/translated WCs, is now checked from the same physical builder as the render; other furniture retains its existing framing contract.
- Control tier 1: `render_views.choose` returns measured horizontal/vertical constraints for every lens decision, gives fully framed candidates strict priority over aesthetic score, projects all WC vertices and refines failed whole-WC searches from 0.10 to 0.025 m. `villa_render.VIEWS` records structured `lens_basis` for both lenses without view-identifier branches and passes the intended eye height. Tier 2: `subject_mesh_frame_violations` checks the exported built WC against both image dimensions; `test_camera_24mm_level_at_eye_height` requires zero fitting 24 mm candidates, an exceeded actual constraint and all 16 mm constraints satisfied. No assertion threshold or fixture pose changed.
- Proofs: `test_frozen_east_wc_vertical_lens_need_and_whole_mesh` fires on the actual former clipping, stays quiet on the corrected camera, and fires on narrower-lens/wrong-aim/missing-mesh mutations. `test_whole_wc_search_generalises_to_renamed_translated_room` proves the same search with other identifiers and a 7 m / -4 m translation. `test_live_east_wc_is_wholly_in_frame` measures exported meshes. The existing guest rain-head regression is the upper-vertical sibling. The first translated-proof run exposed a test-harness assumption that all bath-fitting records had point coordinates; it now preserves non-point zone/line records and translates the point records consumed by the search.
- Checkpoint 2: inspected `out/c4-family-wc-camera-preview.png`, a diagnostic projection of actual unchanged WC meshes (not a photoreal render). New position [9.602, -24.846, 1.35], target [12.200, -26.346, 1.35], lens 16 mm, zero shift. Whole vertical need 36.62 degrees / limit 36.87; the refined 24 mm best needs 36.30 / 26.57. The fixed design is unchanged; presentation image review remains separate.
- Registry: `knowledge/mounting-guards.json` / `c4-lens-basis-missing-vertical`; workflow: `.agents/skills/villa-render/SKILL.md`. Final requested check exits and evidence: `docs/c4-family-wc-lens-report.md`.
- Final scope proof repeats the known coordinate-container trap if a JSON-frozen layout is compared directly with Python tuples. Normalizing serialization before value comparison proves every fixture-layout value unchanged; the 204 unique exported WC vertices are exactly equal to the frozen physical geometry. Evidence: `out/c4-family-wc-unchanged-design.log`; this comparison procedure follows the existing container-normalization guard rather than relaxing geometry equality.

## Process lessons (2026-10-06)

### ssh-monitor-silent-drop — long-lived remote wait connections failed silently
- Observed: 2026-10-06 (lead error), monitor scripts holding a single persistent `ssh ... until` connection went silent when the remote network connection dropped. Two monitors expired with no event or notification while both workstation jobs had actually finished ~1.5 hours earlier.
- Found by / stage: Lead inspection of remote workstation state during Stage 7 batch rendering / workstation job runs; should have been caught immediately upon connection drop.
- Reproduction: A remote monitoring command executed over a single long-lived SSH session (`ssh <host> 'until ...; do sleep 10; done'`) hangs indefinitely without event when the transport connection drops without clean socket termination.
- Direct cause: Monitor scripts relied on a single long-lived remote SSH connection to wait for job completion, rather than polling local loop iterations with short, bounded connection timeouts.
- Escape: Dropped SSH connection produced no local event or retry in the monitor; no per-call timeout or client-side heartbeat existed.
- Class: Proposed class `remote execution monitor fragile` (or mapped to `execution context implicit` / `pipeline result lacks atomic proof`). Siblings: any long-lived remote monitoring connection across workstation jobs or cloud execution.
- Control tier: Tier 3 process-only rule. Control status: Process change only (applied fix: poll locally with short `timeout 30 ssh` calls per iteration, never one long-lived SSH wait; documented in `docs/ops/agent-dispatch.md`). No automated watchdog guard exists yet.
- Proofs: Process verification; remote polling scripts invoke bounded `timeout 30 ssh ...` commands per iteration with explicit local sleep intervals and bounded failure handling.
- Registry: `ssh-monitor-silent-drop` -> `docs/ops/agent-dispatch.md`, `docs/ops/workstation-jobs.md` -> remote workstation monitoring procedure.

### visible-element-unpreviewed-integration — new visible elements referenced in scene without preview artifact
- Observed: 2026-10-06, an autonomous agent (Codex) integrated procedural Strelitzia (which rendered as crude lollipop blobs on bare stalks) and a sloped ramp with paving applied to its soffit directly into the villa scene without generating or inspecting any visual preview artifact. Both defects were caught only later during lead render review.
- Found by / stage: Lead render review at Stage 7 presentation rendering; should have been caught before the builder could be referenced by the scene assembly.
- Reproduction: A newly authored procedural component builder or material mapping is wired into `villa_render.build` or `villa_landscape.build` while the build manifest or candidate output lacks an associated preview artifact path and hash.
- Direct cause: Look-before-integrate existed only as an informal convention and memory/skill rule, but was not enforced by an automated gate requiring a validated preview before scene referencing.
- Escape: Scene assembly builders directly imported and invoked newly authored procedural generators without verifying prior preview generation or approval.
- Class: Proposed class `visual preview gate omitted` (maps to `evidence or scope silently promoted` in `docs/lessons-audit.md` / `human judgement or intent`). Siblings: all new procedural meshes, furniture builders, plantings, and material assignments.
- Control tier: Tier 3 process-only rule. Control status: Process change only (documented in `docs/ops/agent-dispatch.md`: "a new visible element needs a recorded neutral preview (path + hash) before its builder can be referenced by the scene"). Automated fail-closed check at scene reference time is planned / pending, not yet implemented.
- Proofs: Review evidence requires path and SHA-256 hash of neutral preview artifact before integration; review checklist item in `docs/review-steps.md`.
- Registry: `visible-element-unpreviewed-integration` -> `docs/ops/agent-dispatch.md`, `docs/review-steps.md`, `.agents/skills/villa-render/SKILL.md` -> scene integration boundary.

### procedural-plant-and-soffit-guards — crude botanical proxies and downward paving escaped to render
- Observed: 2026-10-06, scene generation integrated procedural Strelitzia that produced crude primitive stand-ins (lollipop blobs on bare stalks) and a sloped ramp with stone paving finish assigned to its downward-facing soffit. Caught only at lead render review.
- Found by / stage: Lead render review at Stage 7 visual verification; should have been caught at builder construction and material assignment.
- Reproduction: Freeze procedural Strelitzia geometry (cylinder stalk with sphere head) and sloped ramp geometry where downward-facing faces ($z < -0.7$) carry paving finishes. Construction-level unit tests assert botanical structure and reject floor finishes on downward normals.
- Direct cause: The procedural Strelitzia generator used crude primitive geometry (spheres on bare stalks) rather than structured botanical morphology; the ramp generator assigned deck paving uniformly across all mesh faces without filtering by normal orientation.
- Escape: Scene construction accepted primitive shapes without botanical form constraints, and surface finish assignment lacked downward normal orientation checks.
- Class: `proxy lacks physical geometry` (for Strelitzia lollipop blobs; audit l0046, l0069) and `appearance lacks verified basis` (for ramp soffit paving; audit l0016, l0028).
- Control tier: Tier 3 human judgement / render review (Item D.6 in `docs/review-steps.md`); Tier 2 construction-level guards (`plant-form` morphology checks and `soffit-material` downward-facing paving rejection in G2f). Control status: Construction-level guards (G2f) are QUEUED / planned, not yet implemented; currently guarded by Tier 3 visual review.
- Proofs: Visual failure captured in lead render review. The G2f plant-form guard (max foliage gap 0.20 m, proven on the frozen 0.66 m Strelitzia gap) and the soffit-material guard (no floor finish on faces with normal z < -0.7; 31 stones and 2 bench pads also fixed) are implemented in commit fc3bf42 on the garden branch agy/c4-plant, not yet merged to main. The preview-artefact gate itself remains a process rule (docs/ops/agent-dispatch.md).
- Registry: `procedural-plant-and-soffit-guards` -> `docs/review-steps.md` (Moment D), `src/archpipe/concept/villa_landscape.py`, `src/archpipe/concept/villa_render.py` -> Stage 7 scene export and review.

### agent-prompt-appended-contradiction — incremental addenda produced self-contradictory dispatch instructions
- Observed: 2026-10-06 (lead error), a dispatch prompt for task G4 grew incrementally via appended addenda and notes, ending in mutually contradictory instructions: an earlier instruction directed the agent to "STOP if no climber record" while a later appended addendum directed it to "add these lead-verified records". Codex correctly halted on the conflicting precondition, costing an entire execution turn.
- Found by / stage: Agent dispatch and execution turn evaluation during Stage 7 landscape task; caught by the agent refusing to proceed.
- Reproduction: Construct an agent instruction prompt with an explicit `STOP if <condition>` guard followed by an appended addendum instructing action under that exact condition; agent stops execution at the earlier guard.
- Direct cause: Prompt text was amended by appending incremental notes to the end rather than rewriting the prompt holistically to reconcile updated decisions with existing preconditions.
- Escape: No pre-dispatch consistency check was performed to verify new addenda against earlier STOP/precondition lines before launching the subagent.
- Class: Proposed class `dispatch instruction contradiction` (or mapped to `human judgement or intent`). Siblings: all multi-stage delegated agent tasks where scope or preconditions evolve mid-flight.
- Control tier: Tier 3 process-only rule. Control status: Process change only (rule: rewrite the whole job prompt when a decision changes; check new text against every STOP/precondition line before dispatch; documented in `docs/ops/agent-dispatch.md`). No automated prompt linter implemented.
- Proofs: Operational verification during task dispatch; pre-dispatch verification checklist in `docs/ops/agent-dispatch.md`.
- Registry: `agent-prompt-appended-contradiction` -> `docs/ops/agent-dispatch.md` -> agent dispatch workflow.

### site-orientation-conflation — true north bearing conflated with client project orientation
- Observed: 2026-10-06 (client feedback), the lead named the street-side garden "west court" and the +y boundary strip "north court" based on the true-north bearing (model +y = 20 deg east of true north) instead of the client's established naming convention (street side = north, model +y = east, model +x = south; aligned with `villa_env` `OFFSET_N/E/S` and client room names). The garden brief and landscape code carried the naming error, prompting immediate client rejection ("WHY ARE YOU GETTING DIRECTION WRONG!"). In the same session, `villa_landscape.direct_sun_hours` was found applying true solar azimuths directly to model coordinate axes without the 20 deg rotation, while using a 3 m enclosure box proxy, reporting 4–5 h of direct sunlight where an accurate scene ray-cast simulation yields approximately 1 h.
- Found by / stage: Client presentation review ("WHY ARE YOU GETTING DIRECTION WRONG!") and subsequent technical audit of solar calculations; should have been caught during site setup and landscape solar validation.
- Reproduction: Run `villa_landscape.direct_sun_hours` on unrotated model axes; compare the resulting 4–5 h against scene ray-casting (~1 h). Inspect landscape zone naming against client brief definitions.
- Direct cause: Geographic true north (used for astronomical solar positioning) was conflated with project nominal north (used for client spatial orientation and coordinate naming); solar calculation omitted the 20 deg coordinate frame rotation and used a crude 3 m proxy enclosure instead of scene geometry ray-casting.
- Escape: Zone naming was authored without validating against client conventions; solar calculations were verified only against synthetic proxy expectations rather than scene ray-casting or independent solar almanac data.
- Class: Proposed class `orientation naming implicit` (or mapped to `coordinate or unit conversion scattered` / `authored values overwritten` in `docs/lessons-audit.md`). Siblings: all solar exposure calculations, facade daylighting assessments, room naming, and garden zone briefs.
- Control tier: Tier 1 (authoritative orientation definition in `knowledge/site-orientation.json` and client naming alignment); Tier 2 (automated compass-word guard on brief/code and scene ray-cast sun calculation replacing proxy model). Control status: QUEUED, not done (Codex job is queued to add `knowledge/site-orientation.json`, rename zones to client naming, replace the sun model with a ray-cast, and add a compass-word guard).
- Proofs: Queued regression testing validating direct sun hours against scene ray-casting and validating compass term usage against `knowledge/site-orientation.json`.
- Registry: `site-orientation-conflation` -> `knowledge/site-orientation.json` (queued), `villa_landscape.py`, `tests/test_landscape.py` -> site orientation and solar analysis boundary.

### emitter-fitting-phase1 — emitter, housing, mounting and photometry disconnected across subsystems
- Observed: 2026-10-06 (Lessons Class C5 audit), luminaire attributes (flux, CCT, emitter coordinate, housing extents, mounting height, and room assignment) were fragmented across five independent authoring boundaries (`villa_lighting.py`, `villa_render.py`, `spec/bedroom-test.yaml`, `extract_model.py`, and `library.py`). In `l0123`, swapping to a 4300 lm lamp set over-lit bedroom targets to 501 lx. In `l0095`, Revit's internal Light Source apex sat at 2242.8 mm while family insertion was 2000 mm. In `l0096`, specifying mounting height 2400 mm pushed shade top through the 2700 mm ceiling. In `l0119`, recessed housing penetrating ceiling void was conflated with geometric clash. In `l0610`, dining fill sitting inside lounge clear rect was mislabelled as dining.
- Found by / stage: Stage 7 lighting verification, luminaire bench sweeps, and Revit extract inspection; should have been caught at luminaire specification and scene assembly.
- Reproduction: Frozen real test cases in `tests/fixtures/c5_failing_case.json` and unit tests in `tests/test_fixture_record.py` reproducing `l0095`, `l0096`, `l0119`, `l0123`, and `l0610` by value, along with translated siblings.
- Direct cause: Lack of a single authoritative data structure owning luminaire photometrics, emitter datums, housing geometry, and mounting contracts; subsystems independently authored and transformed attributes with discordant conventions.
- Escape: Validation checks tested individual properties in isolation without asserting cross-subsystem consistency between authored specs, measured meshes, and bound manufacturer records.
- Class: `emitter and fitting disconnected` (Class C5 in `docs/lessons-audit.md`). Siblings: all 14 Class C5 lessons (`l0025`, `l0026`, `l0074`, `l0080`, `l0090`, `l0095`, `l0096`, `l0119`, `l0123`, `l0606`, `l0610`, `l0650`, `l0656`, `l0874`).
- Control tier: Tier 2 fail-closed consistency check `archpipe.fixture_record.fixture_findings` and guard adapter `check_fixture_record_consistency` registered in `archpipe.guard_registry`. Phase 2 will elevate to Tier 1 prevent-by-construction via unified immutable `FixtureRecord`.
- Proofs: `tests/test_fixture_record.py` (15 tests proving real cases and siblings fire, current villa design and clean baseline stay quiet, unreadable inputs fail closed); `tests/test_guard_registry.py::TestGuardRegistry.test_c5_fixture_record_guard_execution`.
- Registry: `fixture_record_consistency` registered in `src/archpipe/guard_registry.py` covering `l0095`, `l0096`, `l0119`, `l0123`, `l0610`.
### appearance-basis-phase1 — visible surfaces and models lacked verified optical basis
- Observed: 2026-10-06 (Lessons audit C7 Phase 1), multiple visible surfaces and materials across the villa render suite had unverified optical parameters: saturated pure CAD display colors (magenta trellis box l0016, pure-red lamp shade l0064, uncalibrated dark bronze rendering as pale tan l0084, low-reflectance bedding rendering grey l0065, pink tinted stone/wood l0677), unphysical reflectance values, missing texture real-world dimensions (artificial-grass rendering as flat mint plane l0891), degenerate wood grain texture coordinate orientation (oak grain running horizontally across wardrobe gaps l0083 and Box-projection texel streaking on horizontal planes l0795), missing glass optical records or unhandled double-interface slab transmission (extracted glass solid applying whole-window transmission twice l0049, bath screen reading as mirror l0910), translucent opal diffuser globes reading as smoky grey glass due to missing transmission target (l0900), and emissive exitance/CCT disagreement (whole room rendering orange l0062).
- Found by / stage: Comprehensive C7 audit across `src/archpipe/concept/villa_render.py`, `src/archpipe/blender/villa_scene.py`, `src/archpipe/concept/villa_landscape.py`, `revit_spec.py`, and the texture asset library manifest.
- Reproduction: Frozen real failure fixtures for 12 historical defects: `l0016` (saturated magenta CAD box), `l0028` (Revit raw viewport hue substituted for measured reflectance), `l0049` (glass solid lacking declared transmittance or interface count), `l0062` (emissive blackbody CCT / exitance contradiction), `l0064` (saturated pure red CAD lamp shade), `l0065` (ivory bedding with low reflectance < 0.20), `l0083` (wood grain horizontal axis on vertical face), `l0084` (dark bronze with high unphysical albedo > 0.40), `l0795` (wood grain box coordinate rotation collapsing span on horizontal member), `l0891` (artificial grass without real-world texture scale), `l0900` (translucent diffuser globe transmittance <= 0.40), `l0910` (single-interface open glass mesh); plus renamed/translated siblings for each real case.
- Direct cause: Materials and shader parameters were authored procedurally or imported from CAD display colors without binding to verified product cut sheets, laboratory reflectance/transmittance curves, or real-world physical dimensions.
- Escape: Visual QA relied on eye inspection or incomplete post-render pixel checks without verifying optical inputs and texture coordinates at the scene definition stage.
- Class: `appearance lacks verified basis` (docs/lessons-audit.md Class C7). Siblings: all 54 scene materials in `villa_render.py`, procedural detail elements in `revit_spec.py`, texture coordinate orientations, and asset library textures.
- Control tier: Tier 2 automated fail-closed check `archpipe.material_basis.material_findings` and `assert_material_basis`, registered as guard `appearance_basis_phase1` in `src/archpipe/guard_registry.py`. Calls production authorities `archpipe.concept.villa_render.M`, `archpipe.blender.grain.mapping_rotated_span`, `archpipe.blender.build_scene.kelvin_to_rgb`, and `archpipe.villa_render_contract.emission_strength`.
- Baseline and proofs: `tests/test_material_basis.py` proves detection on all 12 frozen historical defects and renamed/translated siblings. Current active production materials stay quiet under `assert_material_basis` (0 ERROR findings) and match `tests/fixtures/c7_known_findings.json` (5 WARN findings on walnut/oak/door-oak horizontal grain orientation, brass missing alloy cut sheet, and glass-guard missing IOR specification). `tests/test_guard_registry.py` verifies registration, real firing, clean quietness, and AST production authority calls.
- Registry: Guard `appearance_basis_phase1` in `src/archpipe/guard_registry.py` covering 14 lesson IDs (`l0016`, `l0028`, `l0049`, `l0062`, `l0064`, `l0065`, `l0083`, `l0084`, `l0677`, `l0724`, `l0795`, `l0891`, `l0900`, `l0910`). Inventory documented in `docs/material-basis.md`.

### 2026-10-07 — refactor-silent-deletion — behaviour-preserving refactor silently dropped assignment and call
- Observed: 2026-10-07, while migrating 16 scripts to the shared launch preflight (commit 13726cc), an agent deleted the line `rendered = json.loads(a.rendered.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))` from `scripts/compare_lux.py` although the task instructed not to change what any script computes. Only two downstream pipeline tests caught the deletion (fixed in a2ae9f8). A manual audit by the lead comparing assigned names across all 16 scripts found that `compare_lux.py` lost `rendered` (real defect) while `demo_bedroom_lighting.py` lost `IES` (harmless rename to `ies`).
- Found by / stage: Downstream pipeline regression tests and lead manual code audit; should have been caught immediately upon refactor completion prior to commit.
- Reproduction: Compare assigned names and call targets per function scope between base and refactored versions; `scripts/compare_lux.py` main() loses assigned name `rendered` and call target `json.loads`.
- Direct cause: Refactoring agent rewrote script entry point to wrap code into `main()` and invoke `project_context` preflight but inadvertently deleted an intermediate data-loading assignment.
- Escape: The refactor was declared behaviour-preserving without an automated AST semantic comparison verifying that all assigned names, call targets, and scopes were preserved, moved, or renamed.
- Class: Proposed class `refactor code deletion` (maps to `authored values overwritten` or `pipeline result lacks atomic proof`). Siblings: dropped stage result writes (`write_stage_result`), dropped intermediate data parsing calls, dropped computations across any refactored script.
- Control tier: Tier 2 automated fail-closed AST audit `archpipe.refactor_audit.audit_source` and CLI `scripts/refactor_audit.py`, registered as guard `refactor_silent_deletion` in `src/archpipe/guard_registry.py`. Tier 3 operational rule in `docs/ops/agent-dispatch.md`.
- Proofs: `tests/test_refactor_audit.py` (real defect in `compare_lux.py` with deleted `rendered = json.loads(...)` fires with one finding naming `rendered` and `json.loads`; real harmless case in `demo_bedroom_lighting.py` with `IES` moved/renamed stays quiet; siblings with renamed copy, dropped `write_stage_result` call without assignment fire; allowed removal with reason stays quiet; unparsable syntax raises).
- Registry: Guard `refactor_silent_deletion` registered in `src/archpipe/guard_registry.py`; operational rule in `docs/ops/agent-dispatch.md`.
### garden-g1-authority — approved palette and east-only brief were not scene contracts
- Capture (G1 diagnosis, 2026-10-06): the pre-change D1 east garden contains two trees (including excluded Bauhinia), boundary beds, trellises, pots, bistro and egg swing. Species sizes/care are distributed over SPREAD, CLUMP_SPREAD, EXTRA_CARE and a shared generated palette; several props have no species identity. Frozen pre-change input is retained in the G1 regression fixture.
- Direct cause: independent dressing recipes remained authoritative after the client brief changed. Escape: geometry/spacing checks did not check approved identity, zone contents or evidence-backed dimensions. Class: a design contract split over unvalidated local recipes and external generated data. Siblings: north/west and top recipes, procedural climbers, door pots and scene provenance.
- Checkpoint 1: at uniform 4.6 m height the measured frangipani model is 6.456 x 5.877 m; the east court is 5.960 m to the plot edge, 5.710 m to the rear fence inner face. The verified 4.6 m mature spread fits. The model and old route guard conflict with the brief; the old layer guard also requires an east bed. Do not shrink evidence or weaken these guards.
- Control tier 1: `knowledge/garden-palette.json` is the single botanical/placement-size record; plant construction rejects unknown/excluded asset identities before placement. East turf is constructed around a real opening, and the tree anchors its measured trunk base rather than its asymmetric canopy box. Shared generated palette data and botanical constants are retired. Scene provenance hashes the tracked record.
- Tier 2: scene export unconditionally checks `species_violations`, `dimension_violations`, `east_content_violations` and `canopy_violations` alongside the unchanged extent, route, layer, spacing, drift, swing, bench and stand-in guards. `review_candidate` reports unresolved conflicts for diagnosis; it is not an export bypass.
- Reproduction/proofs: `tests/fixtures/garden-g1-before.json` freezes the real old props/east surfaces. `tests.test_landscape` fires on old excluded Bauhinia, old 2.3 m frangipani and forbidden east objects; stays quiet on candidate identity/dimensions/contents; generalises to renamed real furniture, excluded Pennisetum, oversized/nonuniform trees and overgrown procedural climbers. The existing geometric guards retain their frozen negative cases.
- Additional first-fix findings: the measured trunk would fall outside a pit placed at the canopy box centre; `test_trunk_centred_and_turf_does_not_cap_assumed_pit` catches that real asset. A centroid/vertex-only turf check missed the frozen old covering quad because its centroid was outside the pit; polygon containment now catches it and stays quiet on the annulus. Class: an envelope/centroid was substituted for the physical functional point or occupied polygon. Future tree-pit assets require a measured trunk anchor, not an assumed model origin.
- Checkpoint 2 / validation: 90 landscape/provenance/climber/exterior-mounting/intake/concept tests exit 0. `test_render_standard`, scene-dependent mounting/view tests, portable verification and support acceptance are blocked by the preserved garden export guard. The measured model exceeds the plot by 0.848 m after centring the trunk, overlaps the living-east route, and old layer requirements remain incompatible. Detailed evidence/assumptions and affected lead views are in `docs/garden-g1-report.md`.
- Registry: `garden-g1-authority` -> authoritative record and the four `villa_landscape` guards -> `tests.test_landscape` palette/identity/content/dimension/trunk/canopy/export proofs plus `tests.test_villa_scene_provenance.test_data_change_is_stale_and_override_is_labelled` -> Stage 7 scene construction (`build`, called unconditionally by `verify.py`) and lead preview review.
- Workflow: future G2/G3 authoring must use the same tracked record, record assumptions explicitly, and preserve rejection of physical conflicts. Do not restore local botanical constants, relabel an unapproved asset, or bypass export to render the diagnostic candidate.
- Status: OPEN. Asset/route/layer conflict resolution and lead preview review remain pending. No render or real design gate is approved by this record.


### garden-g2-walking-envelope — a route is a volume, not the whole canopy footprint
- Capture / checkpoint 1 (2026-10-06): G1 used the full prop footprint for walking. D2 requests real geometry from ground to 2.0 m, the passage height in `render_support.blocked_openings`. D1 changes frangipani scale to 4.6 m maximum canopy span; D3 removes the nonexistent east border from bed scope. Lead decisions and reasons are recorded in the sole palette.
- Cause / escape / class: route occupancy was conflated with property-boundary extent, while border scope and historical mounting approvals were fixed lists independent of actual content. The export guard correctly stopped the first G1 conflict, but box arithmetic could not distinguish high canopy from low branches. Siblings: all plant/furniture props, procedural clumps, pots, all bed scopes, changed trellises and retired camera subjects.
- Tier 1 construction: read actual glTF indexed triangles through every node transform, convert axes and reproduce the Blender floor-seat/scale/yaw transform. Cache by file signatures; do not admit missing/unsupported geometry on an overlapping route. Derive layer scope from the plan's beds. Author north/west trellises at their finite wall-face datums; retire obsolete approvals as explicit data, preserving original coordinates for frozen proofs. Hash the revised approval record in scene provenance.
- Tier 2 detection: intersection of mesh triangles with each walking volume applies to every prop and procedural plant, not a tree ID exemption. `build()` retains unconditional candidate guards. The resized tree has 174 intersecting branch/leaf triangles; lowest candidate triangle height is about 1.713 m above ground. A 25 mm bounded grid examined 649 offsets within the permitted 0.5 m shift and found no clear placement. The trunk remains centred; no mesh pruning, rotation, nonuniform scale or camera-driven design move was used.
- Proofs: current/0.4 m lowered real tree fire; diagnostic raised geometry clears the walking band, and renamed non-tree overhead/low members generalise. The frozen teak-sofa obstruction still fires. New bed data with no layers fails; two actual three-layer beds stay quiet. Six-member drifts fail while five stay quiet. Full-sun Callistemon/Ursinia on the real four-hour west site fail; a nine-hour north site stays quiet. Recorded clump size and doubled-height mutations cover procedural plants. Frozen east mounting drift, south 135.66 mm mismatch and removed lemon/olive camera proofs remain independent of current content.
- First-fix checkpoint: the metre-scale leaf constructor initially called a millimetre furniture helper; its minimum radius produced degenerate stems. It now constructs closed metre-scale petioles directly and transforms fresh coordinates, avoiding repeated transforms of shared ring vertices. Real clumps pass closed/outward geometry and palette dimensions; altered sibling heights fail.
- Door-pot sibling diagnosis: a first near-threshold position cleared the 0.914 m landscape route but entered the wider actual door passage. All four north pots now stand on the turf strip outside the bed and doorway volumes; whole-scene diagnostic `blocked_openings` and `unsupported` are empty. This diagnostic retains the invalid tree and is not scene-export acceptance.
- Registry: `garden-g2-walking-envelope` in `knowledge/mounting-guards.json` -> `route_geometry`, `villa_landscape`, retired approval data and scene provenance -> `tests.test_landscape`, `tests.test_final_mounting`, `tests.test_support_mounting`, `tests.test_render_views`, `tests.test_villa_scene_provenance` -> scene construction/portable verification/lead review.
- Status at G2 checkpoint: OPEN. Authoritative scene construction/portable verification still refused the low branch. No render, preview, commit or real design-gate approval. See `docs/garden-g2-report.md` for the measured handoff. G1 conflict prose is historical; this record supersedes its canopy and layer diagnosis, not its retained frozen evidence.


### garden-g2-clump-contract — part checks alone admitted curved quads and a lighting-key collision
- Capture: the first G2 diagnostic assembly passed `Part` closure/dimensions but the render contract rejected 1,680 nonplanar leaf faces and twelve invalid lighting-layer fields. The actual first Strelitzia clump is frozen by value in `tests/fixtures/garden-g2-clump-before.json` before reconstruction.
- Root / escape / class: closed ring lofts were treated as planar faces, and plant strata reused the renderer's lighting `layer` key. Part checks prove solid closure, not the whole renderer's schema. Siblings: both new leaf species, fourteen ground/pot clumps, and every plant's metadata.
- Tier 1: triangulate swept blade faces when authored; transform fresh coordinates; emit `planting_layer` separately. Metre-scale petioles have their own closed constructor, avoiding a millimetre helper's radius floor. No render-side cleanup or silently hidden material is used.
- Proofs: `test_real_clump_nonplanar_and_lighting_namespace_defects_cannot_recur` fires on the real frozen clump; both current species have only triangles and no mesh contract errors; a renamed old-field mutation fails. Dimension mutations and `Part` closure proofs run on real clumps. The final in-memory diagnostic suite passes 82 checks and has no unsupported part or blocked opening, while authoritative export remains blocked by the independent tree.

Tier 2: the Blender-free test extracts and executes the actual function and actual render-call expression, stubbing only camera projection/context and supplying imported-record/vertex objects. This is not a substitute for a Blender run.

Registry: `garden-g2-imported-qa-wiring` -> render/subjects call -> `tests/test_garden_render_subjects` -> consumer wiring before preview. ### asset-route-build-host-input — scene build read the render host's asset library
- Observed: lead Windows scene build on ec0bc39 raised FileNotFoundError for `sf_frangipani/model.gltf` in `route_geometry.prop_triangles`; the Linux workstation suite passed with its installed library.
- Found by / stage: lead at scene build; should have been caught at portable build regression.
- Direct cause: walking-envelope geometry was read through `Path.home()` from an untracked, machine-local render library.
- Escape: all build tests inherited the workstation's populated HOME; no absent-library reproduction exercised the execution boundary.
- Class: execution context implicit / hidden machine-local calculation input. Related: `lighting-product-hidden-state`.
- Siblings: landscape route checks and `scripts/garden_tree_position.py` both use `prop_triangles`. Blender importers legitimately read the library on the render host; explicit asset intake can measure a supplied model path. `sources.py` names a separate held-book store and is not a scene-build asset consumer.
- Control tier: 1 prevent by construction, plus 2 fail closed at scene build and portable verification.
- Control: `asset_route_record.recorded_triangles` reads only the tracked `knowledge/asset-route-geometry.json` and manifest. Historical first fix retained all 456,334 triangles in a 7,238,188-byte record; the bounded follow-up below supersedes that storage scheme while preserving portability. `scripts/generate_asset_route_geometry.py` measures the real render-host glTF with the existing reader (moved to host-only `asset_route_generator`), records command/date, and writes matching glTF and external-buffer SHA-256 hashes into the record and manifest. SHA-256 is the content digest used to detect changed source bytes. Landscape route checks require a valid record even when a placed prop overlaps no route.
- Proofs: `tests/test_asset_route_geometry.py::test_frozen_ec0bc39_fails_with_empty_home_and_scene_now_builds` executes the entire old module frozen by value and reproduces FileNotFoundError, then builds the current scene without the library. Missing file/asset, changed model/buffer hash and corrupt payload fail with the asset and generator command; a nonoverlapping renamed bench fails too. Generator round-trip on an independent transformed, full-height asset is exact; buffer-only replacement changes provenance. All seven real generated records independently matched the raw reader triangle-for-triangle. Existing landscape tests preserve the teak sofa, lowered/raised frangipani and renamed member proofs, plus the continuous translation solution.
- Prevention guard: `build_input_guard.build_input_findings`, run by `scripts/verify.py`, scans build sources for HOME, library glTF paths and imports of the host-only raw reader. `test_static_guard_real_old_path_siblings_and_clean_build` fires on the real old module and three renamed sibling mutations and stays quiet on clean sources and the downstream library declaration. Explicit caller-configured photometry paths remain distinct from implicit host inputs.
- Workflow: regenerate record and manifest together on the render host after adding/replacing guarded assets; run portable build tests with HOME pointing at an empty directory. The villa-render skill and its generated adapter carry this boundary. No asset-library or shared data was modified; no rendering or native-model acceptance claimed.
- Registry: `knowledge/build-input-guards.json::asset-route-build-host-input`.
- Acceptance (2026-10-06): the eight requested focused modules ran 125 tests with normal HOME and 125 with HOME initially empty; both exited 0. `scripts/verify.py --portable` exited 0 in both environments. The frozen old call fails only in the deliberately absent-library case; the current complete scene builds there. Full-suite execution remains with the lead.

### asset-route-record-unbounded — portable record retained unused walking geometry
- Observed / found by: lead follow-up found the uncommitted record was 7,238,188 bytes for 456,334 triangles; should have been bounded during build-input generation.
- Cause / escape: the generator retained whole assets to support arbitrary scales, with no consumer audit, recorded scale range or size regression. Class: derived build input has no declared consumer envelope. Siblings: walking collision, translation search and view framing share the record; framing requires full-height vertices. The authored bench uses independent axis scales, unlike the uniformly scaled plants.
- Diagnosis checkpoint: six assets are entirely below the walking top at the build scales. Only frangipani can lose triangles without changing walking evidence; a sub-1 MB target cannot be obtained simply by applying a walking band. Preserve exact geometry and measure lossless storage instead of pruning obstructing branches.
- Construction / control tier: 1, generator filters exact triangles intersecting an authored base-relative band; 2, build validates positive scale intervals and actual walking tops. The band adds 0.41 m world coverage to the existing 2.0 m height, divided by minimum allowed scale in native units. No triangle decimation or rounding. Framing uses full-height vertices without above-band faces. The bench preserves its recorded axiswise exception.
- Proofs: `test_exact_band_boundary_and_scale_range_generalise` keeps a just-inside and crossing triangle exactly, drops a just-above triangle, preserves transformed full-height framing points, rejects out-of-range/unsupported scales and uncovered walking tops, and stays quiet at both scale endpoints. `test_real_scale_and_lowering_outside_band_fail_closed` fires on frangipani and ixora and on excessive lowering. `test_record_storage_contract` fixes the measured before/after totals and detects re-expansion. The first route/landscape package passed 36 tests, exit 0, preserving D2 sofa, lowered/raised frangipani and renamed-member proofs.
- Independent source check: all seven retained triangle arrays match raw render-host source filtered by the declared band exactly; all framing vertices match raw full-height vertices exactly. 456,334 -> 421,178 triangles; frangipani 82,691 -> 47,535. Record 7,238,188 -> 5,919,050 bytes. Six assets are wholly within the walking band; the well-under-1 MB target remains unmet with this lossless encoding. No exact walking geometry was discarded to hit a size target.
- Registry / workflow: `knowledge/build-input-guards.json::asset-route-record-unbounded`; `verify.py` validates every recorded band and size reduction. Canonical villa-render skill and generated adapter updated together. Full reader audit, per-asset counts/coverage, size limitation and repeatable procedure: [asset-route-band.md](asset-route-band.md).
- Final acceptance (2026-10-06): 172 focused tests with normal HOME and 172 with HOME initially empty both exited 0. Final `verify.py --portable` exited 0 in both environments; generated adapter check and `git diff --check` exited 0. No render or commit. Full suite remains with the lead; the storage target limitation remains explicit.

### asset-route-record-size-budget — exact triangle topology needs a compact representation
- Observed / lead follow-up: the walking-band record still occupies 5,919,050 bytes, above the requested 1.5 MB budget; capture: prior record size and counts in `docs/asset-route-band.md`.
- Cause / escape / class: full-height framing and walking geometry shared one vertex pool, and lexicographic vertex numbering made adjacent triangle indices expensive. The size check only required improvement over an older record. Class: derived data lacks separate consumer representations and an absolute storage budget. Siblings: route checking, continuous translation search, and camera framing.
- Diagnosis checkpoint: framing needs only original 3D convex-hull vertices, with unchanged coordinates. Walking retains every selected triangle and its original winding/connectivity. Frangipani's micrometre translation separation requires lossless coordinates; the other assets can use the explicitly permitted millimetre scene precision. Hull reduction never applies to walking geometry.
- Control tier 1: separate unrounded full-height hull vertices from first-use indexed walking vertices; preserve every selected triangle and its order/winding/connectivity. Frangipani stays bit-for-bit float64 for the existing micrometre translation separation; six other assets use the permitted integer scene grid (at most 1 mm step at maximum scale), with Euclidean vertex error bounded by 0.866026 mm. The original base and pre-quantisation band selection remain unchanged. Byte-shuffled LZMA payloads inside deterministic gzip are lossless at that declared precision.
- Tier 2: the generator refuses a record of 1,500,000 bytes or more before writing record or manifest; `verify.py --portable` enforces the same budget. The payload digest binds the independent hull, precision and coverage metadata; missing/corrupt gzip, vertices, hulls and changed precision fail closed with the asset and generator command.
- First-fix checkpoint: 421,178 walking triangles retained; 267,020 full-height source vertices reduce to 1,098 hull vertices. Record 5,919,050 -> 1,482,582 bytes. Fresh raw-source audit of all seven assets matches their declared indexed encoding exactly; maximum measured scene error is 0.853689 mm, with zero for frangipani. Fresh all-vertex vs hull comparisons on real frangipani and swing agree for in-frame, top/bottom/horizontal clipping and behind-camera cases.
- Proofs: `test_real_hull_only_matches_all_vertex_frame_results` compares frozen full-height source results tied to source/buffer hashes; `scripts/generate_asset_framing_proof.py --check` repeats the comparison from actual source vertices without rendering. `test_quantised_branch_one_mm_inside_stays_detected` proves the real integer round-trip retains face order and detects a branch 1 mm inside, stays quiet 1 mm outside, and rejects excessive error even with a rebound digest. `test_hull_degenerate_and_transformed_siblings_generalise` covers point/line/plane geometry, unrelated coordinates, linear transforms and near-boundary halfspaces. `test_size_budget_refuses_real_record_before_writing` fires on the real current record under an insufficient budget and preserves old bytes; the clean 1.5 MB budget passes. Existing missing/stale/corrupt, empty-HOME build, exact continuous-tree translation and 0.01 mm inward-movement proofs remain.
- First-fix harness corrections: the renamed synthetic branch has no catalogue bounding box, so its broad-phase rectangle is measured from its decoded triangles. Direct proof-script invocation initially lacked the repository root on Python's module path; the entry point now resolves that root explicitly, and the real `--check` command passes. These corrections do not change source geometry or camera/design inputs.
- Registry / workflow: `knowledge/build-input-guards.json::asset-route-record-size-budget`; canonical villa-render skill and generated adapter describe the separate framing hull, precision, budget and fresh source proof. [asset-route-band.md](asset-route-band.md) records the current representation; older 5.9 MB notes and logs are historical.
- Final acceptance (2026-10-06): 176 focused tests with normal HOME passed in 225.748 seconds; the same 176 with initially empty HOME passed in 224.926 seconds. Both processes exited 0. `NO_COLOR=1` portable verification exited 0 in both environments. Fresh real-source hull comparison, all-seven-asset precision audit, generated adapter check and `git diff --check` passed. Logs: `/tmp/asset-route-compact-*`. No render, commit or full-suite result; the lead owns the full suite.

New controls and proofs are recorded after first-fix validation below; photographic review remains with the lead.

Actual outer faces, including rims and shoots, now drive top outline/rail/route checks. Full bottom-face seating prevents a tilted trough from passing on one corner. The unchanged sloping driveway has a segment-by-segment D2 walking check through actual triangles. Control tiers 1 (shared datums/construction) and 2 (unconditional built geometry guards).
- Proofs: `test_g3_actual_rim_reproduction_surface_route_and_renamed_siblings` fires on the real old rim, renamed sibling, raised/sunk/tilted/edge/route trough and trailing plant, stays quiet on the built candidate, generalises to translated coordinates and a nonzero datum, and proves build refusal. `test_g3_trough_drifts_seating_palette_and_material_intent` checks drifts, soil roots, slab seating and knee-side facing. `test_g3_study_gate_chain_and_sloped_d2_volume_keep_driveway` checks the real ramp/profile and independent slope/floor/beam siblings. The measured plan preview `out/garden-g3-plan.png` is inspected; this does not replace the lead's close-up photoreal preview.
- Siblings migrated: top trough bodies/soil, both pot/rim assemblies, rosemary/aloe shoots, bench slabs/seats, and street-gate access. Palette remains the sole species/dimension authority. First full-scene contract, `unsupported` and `blocked_openings` are clear. Numerical validation is distinct from open supplier, nursery, colour and lead photographic decisions.

### garden-g3-solid-leaf-tip — taper collapsed a succulent cap

Siblings: procedural aloe leaves, broad Strelitzia/Aspidistra blades and rosemary needle tubes; existing broad blades and tubes retain nonzero endpoint thickness.

Existing G2 real clump contract test now covers the nine new top clumps as well. Preview: inspected measured top plan; nursery likeness and lead close-up render remain open.
- Registry: `knowledge/mounting-guards.json::garden-g3-solid-leaf-tip`; workflow `docs/ops/garden-rebuild.md` requires endpoint thickness and full isolated contract checks before preview. No photographic closure claimed.

Frozen scene vertices/camera: `tests/fixtures/garden-g6b-upper-frame-before.json.gz`; image `out/garden-g6b/v27-landscape-cropped-neutral.png` before camera correction. Actual vertical extent 0.7389477066 sensor-width units exceeds the landscape frame height 0.6666666667. The inherited G6 frame had only 2.226 pixels of margin; new rounded shrub leaves occupy more real depth near the camera without moving roots/envelopes. The neutral consumer's subject receipt was insufficient to prove every actual vertical vertex, and an initial independent review inferred full framing from it. Class: changed geometry inherits an appearance-approved but tight camera, with a weaker delivered receipt than the deterministic framing requirement. Siblings: updated plants, cushion anatomy, portrait previews and new camera identifiers.
- Construction control: preserve fixed position, supported floor, target and 24 mm level lens; physically rotate the same 36 × 24 mm sensor to portrait and measure the new shift from actual vertical extremes. Require full-subject intent at authoring so authoritative build/contract/portable verification refuse a cropped upper view. The portrait candidate passes all actual vertices, unchanged 1 m proximity and native standing support; three target rays are 12/13, 12/13 and 13/13. Checkpoint 2: independent actual portrait preview review accepted the complete three-layer grouping (`out/garden-g6b/context-critic-review.md`); integrate only this accepted aspect correction. Frozen real crop and renamed/translated siblings fail; corrected current portrait stays quiet (`test_real_upper_crop_refused_current_portrait_and_translated_sibling`). No planting moved or shrunk.
- Delivery wrapper escape: an inline diagnostic called `main()` without returning its status, so shell exit zero accompanied the clipping message. `scripts/villa_render_views.py --output` now supplies a direct supported CLI that returns its existing failure status. Run the real frozen/current exports through this CLI; do not infer acceptance from the inline wrapper or receipt's framed flag. The command, actual image, physical projection and independent review must agree.

### garden-g6b-test-edit — ambiguous patch context split an existing method
- Capture: two test insertions matched a repeated internal assertion instead of the next method boundary. The first was caught by read-back; the second real file failed `py_compile` with unexpected indentation (`out/garden-g6b/test-edit-parse-before.py` and its log). Class: a context patch splices a method before its existing tail. Use a complete method boundary when adding a sibling, inspect both surrounding methods, and compile all edited Python files before launching costly acceptance. Actual broken file refuses; repaired current files compile with exit 0 (`test-edit-parse-after.log`). ### garden-g6b-view-search-limit — conservative imported bounds are not exact surface distance
- Additional bounded diagnostic: `out/garden-g6b/hull-camera-screen.json` preserves the real frangipani, its recorded full-height hull and ten camera poses. At (28.300, −27.000, −1.650) m the bounding-box screen reports 0.778 m, while a separating plane through the recorded hull proves at least 1.259 m geometric separation. The plane's unit normal points from the hull bounding centre toward the camera; the minimum signed projection over all hull vertices is a conservative distance lower bound for the enclosed actual geometry. This identifies conservatism, not an exact nearest-surface measurement. Do not describe a box bound as measured surface proximity.

- Identity boundary: early staged reconstruction passed an already canonical `landscape-` identifier to `_mesh`, which adds that prefix itself. Construction now accepts both short and canonical names at the new shared-builder boundary; the real-case sibling test proves identical complete records for both forms and quiet translated other-villa specimens. This prevents a staging-only identity from masquerading as the actual scene subject.
- Finish-register sibling: the broad focused run found the historical G6b omission test's literal missing-finish set lacked the new `aspidistra-leaf`. Its expected omission set now includes that real finish, and the omission / arbitrary renamed-replacement loop covers it alongside both G4d finishes. The independent current finish register remains authoritative; the guard still refuses unknown replacements. G6b is rerun after this test-expectation update.

### garden-g4e-evening-view — beam bounds and framing did not establish delivered appearance
- Capture: lead 2026-10-08 retained evening image has 99.5th percentile 0.76 below 0.9 and cool cast 0.105 above 0.02; fixture body is a foreground subject. Frozen view, four fixtures, sky and mineral/foliage definitions share `garden-g4e-before.json.gz`; retained linear EXR remains read-only on workstation.
- Checkpoint 1: sky is 30 lux Belfast sunset, lamps warm 2700 K with 120 source / approximately 93 shielded lumens. Previous cone vertex counts ignored first-hit foliage interception; framing did not reject foreground fittings. Same prefix v41 also names the south terrace. Class: authored target/coarse bounds and identifier strings were accepted without physical delivery or numeric uniqueness checks.
- Diagnosis: production-glass linear probes show sky-only / combined mean luminance 0.75617 / 0.77119 on gravel and 0.68886 / 0.70449 on walls. The HDRI horizontal-weighted blue/red ratio is 1.379; 2700 K lamp ratio is 0.0996. Black-gravel diagnostic lowers wall sky luminance about 7.4% and foliage about 11%; gravel amplifies the sky rather than generating the blue. Uplights-only foliage mean is 0.28951; the original lamps are warm and reach foliage. Source-isolation is approximate, not new presentation QA. Locked exposure remains -1.27043736 stops; no sky, finish, flux, beam, aim or QA limit was changed to compensate.
- Tier 1 / 2: camera-only search with complete framing, actual first-hit subject and fitting visibility, standing-domain, clearance and opening-frame checks; unique numeric view prefixes; v42 records its v41 alias. The original foreground fitting fails, final v42 and renamed sibling stay quiet, old-camera mutation fires; duplicate v41 and v07 prefixes fail. Non-garden cameras remain quiet. Proofs: `tests/test_garden_g4e.py::EveningScope`; register `knowledge/garden-render-guards.json`. Context before/after in `out/garden-g4e/context-production-before`, `context-production-after`, `context-production-v37-after`.
- Open lighting defect: retained production QA reproduces cool cast 0.105 > 0.02, and highlight percentile 0.76 < 0.9 (current unchanged locked-exposure policy classifies highlights WARN). New v42 is an honest through-glazing rear-basket view, with weaker visible foliage uplight coverage in the linear probe. Neither source diagnosis nor neutral preview closes the presentation lighting failure. Eliminating the sky-driven cast would require a separately authorised sky/lighting decision; aim-only changes cannot remove the measured source. Full report: `docs/garden-g4e-report.md`.
- Historical fixture integration trap: adding real first-hit fitting rays exposed missing unchanged `black-metal` definitions in the lean G4d geometry fixture. The initial test repair completed the positive context only; its negative camera mutation still used the raw fixture and reproduced the same lookup error. Every physical-ray variant now uses the same completed scene/material register; renderer guards and limits stay unchanged. The full G4d module is rerun after this test-context correction, with the frozen negative camera still required to fail.

### garden-g4e-neutral-emission — a neutral world left design luminous shaders active
- Capture / checkpoint 1: first garden context previews retained warm light from unrelated emissive fitting materials despite a neutral world and no design light objects. Direct cause: shader emission survives deleting light objects. Escape/class: the diagnostic driver treated world replacement as complete illumination isolation. Siblings include explicit EMISSION nodes and Principled emission, and all future neutral context previews. A separate adapter audit also found diagnostic glass setup omitted production interface transmittance.
- Tier 1 / checkpoint 2: `garden_g6_preview.disable_material_emission` zeros every built/imported luminous shader for context previews, preserves roughness, records diagnostic neutral fill separately, and uses production `configure_glass`. Before and after use the identical recorded white softbox; it never enters design export. Source isolation likewise disables unrelated emitters and applies production glass. Earlier warm/dark previews are superseded by `context-production-*` receipts.
- Proof: actual runtime emission nodes and a Principled sibling become zero, remain zero on repeat/renamed calls, and retain roughness in `tests/test_garden_g4e.py::EveningScope.test_neutral_context_disables_actual_emission_and_principled_sibling`. Production-glass neutral previews and four-mode linear probes were rerun. Control registered in `knowledge/garden-render-guards.json`; workflow and canonical/generated villa-render skills updated together.

### garden-g4f-aspidistra-fountain — small tip drop accepted an upright bundle
- Capture / checkpoint 1: lead rejected all nine G4e Aspidistra on 2026-10-08; `after-all-final/landscape-east-edge-00.png` has 24 narrow upright blades and only 45–58 mm crown-to-tip drop. Real rejected meshes and cameras frozen by value in `tests/fixtures/garden-g4f-rejected.json.gz` before rebuilding.
- Cause / escape / class: anisotropic envelope fitting narrowed blades; many repeated upright arches filled a cylindrical mass. The guard checked only basal distribution and a 25 mm drop, never broadness or outward lean. Local curvature is insufficient evidence of species habit. Siblings: all nine north/east/south clumps through both shared builders; Rhapis is lead-accepted and unchanged. G4f uses the lead's 0.5 m recorded spread rather than the earlier 0.4 m procedural reach. Quantitative habit criteria are authored morphology assumptions, not botanical standards.
- Control and measured proofs: pending G4f preview and focused validation; do not infer lead acceptance from tests.

### garden-g4f-camera-delivery — valid rays accepted the wrong visible subject faces
- Capture / checkpoint 1: lead rejected G4e v42 (uplights-only foliage percentile 0.437 versus original 14.30; rear basket and foreground mullion) and v37 (left wall slab about 30%). Retained previews and probes in `out/garden-g4e`; frozen poses in the G4f rejected fixture.
- Cause / escape / class: camera search minimised displacement once geometric guards passed; it did not compare the luminous foliage population or wall occupation. Siblings: north dusk and lounge views; general camera guards remain unchanged. Neutral context and linear population probes must accompany camera selection. The lead permits only v37's feature stone at 6/13 under denser accepted Rhapis; every other subject remains 7/13. Faithful dusk cool cast is disclosed with its measured source and retained FAIL; highlights retain WARN.
- Control and measured proofs: pending G4f preview and focused validation.
